It answers review round 4's R4-01 and R4-02; it does not reverse that round's verdict, reject, given before it, and it approves nothing: ADR-0014 stays Proposed (D-6), and the product owner's security sign-off, which accepts the marmelroy/Zip advisory's residual risk only once both are closed (plan 23.3, A56), is still pending. Host: the build Mac, macOS 26.6.2 (25G83), Apple M5 Max, Xcode 27.1 (27A9275), XcodeGen 2.46.0. No device was used; no simulator was booted (the linkage runs' iOS builds target a generic iOS Simulator destination); nothing played audio; nothing was pushed, and no git remote was reached (28.1). Times are UTC: the linkage runs were made on 23 October 2026, 16:05-16:10 UTC, 24 October on the build Mac's clock, the date the work's commits carry. *(Dated note, 26 October 2026: until then this section's title said that R4-01 and R4-02 are closed, which the gate summary's item 4 gives only to a change that a review has accepted; no review has accepted this one: the third review of the M0 exit preparation, its finding HON-EP-01; section 33.)* *(Dated note, 26 October 2026: "not reviewed: an agent review reads it next" held when it was written. Five agent reviews have read the branch that holds this change since, at `609ed492`, `83030fba`, `415ae25b`, `fcbd6c89` and `941ac9a2`, and none relayed that it accepts it: the first and the fourth found routes of the same kind that it left (SEC-ZIPGUARD-01 and SEC-ZIPGUARD-03; sections 31 and 34), each answered since by an agent's change, review pending, and the fifth that this section did not say so: its finding HON-EP-07; section 35.)*

### 28.1 What was done

- **The guard** (`Scripts/check-dependency-lock.py`, and the header of `Scripts/ci.d/12-dependency-lock.sh`; ADR-0014 sections 1 and 3):
  - **R4-01:** a new category, `manifest-input`, fails a product `project.yml` when any key or value holds `${` (XcodeGen 2.46.0 replaces it from the environment, since `sylvia_generate` does not pass `--no-env`, and from a target template's name, platform or attributes even with `--no-env`; the YAML escape `\x24{` is the same text once parsed) or when a value begins with `~` (XcodeGen does not take such a path from the project's directory). A product `Package.swift` fails if it reads anything besides its own text: an import other than PackageDescription or CompilerPluginSupport, `Context`, `CommandLine`, `readLine`, `_silgen_name`, `_cdecl` or `_extern`, a random number, a clock, or an `#if`.
  - **R4-02:** product code that is not a test fails when it brings in a file by an absolute name, by a name that climbs out with `..` (unless it is a quoted name that resolves beside the file to a file git tracks under the product roots), or by a path through a build or package-checkout directory, through `#include`, `#import`, `#include_next`, `#embed`, the assembler's `.include` and `.incbin`, or inline assembly; assembly, Metal, OpenCL, lex, yacc, MIG and IIG sources are read as C-family, and a computed include fails too. A build-settings file's `#include` must name a file git tracks under the product roots, so `<DEVELOPER_DIR>/...`, an untracked `#include?`, a path with a variable and a line the check cannot read all fail; and a shipping target may not declare build rules.
  - **R4-03 and R4-04,** two of the same review's minor findings, by the same reasoning: a product manifest or build-settings file may not redefine a build-product, SDK, toolchain or project-directory variable; and `-Werror` or `-Wwarning` takes a diagnostic group only in the Swift compiler's flags.
- **Its self-test:** 278 mutations (239 before) and 35 allowed changes (30 before), and each of the check's 119 failure branches (111 before) reached by the mutation written for it. The repository passes unchanged: no product code uses any of these routes.
- **The routes, reproduced** (`evidence/M0-ExitPrep/zipguard/commands/plant.py` and `reproductions.sh`): each of 23 routes, planted in the real product manifests and code of a copy of the tree, passed the check at `5aec2518` and fails it after the change; the unplanted copy passes both. The same log shows what the tools do with each kind of route: XcodeGen's expansions, with and without `--no-env`; SwiftPM's `dump-package` adding SnapshotTesting to the shipping SylviaDesign target only when an environment variable is set; clang compiling Minizip's own `unzip.c` (Zip 2.1.2) through a quoted and a bracketed `../unzip.c` and a computed include, with Minizip's include directory as the only search path (36 `unz*` functions defined, `_unzOpen` among them); the linker linking a file named `Evil` after `-Werror`; and Xcode following a build-settings file's `#include` outside the project.
- **`resolve-locks.sh` and `linkage.sh` under Xcode 27.1, offline** (`evidence/M0-dependencies/xcode-27.1/`, its README): the agent may not fetch, so both ran unchanged with no git remote reachable (`GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=file`, and a shim that gives `xcodebuild` `-scmProvider system -skipPackageUpdates`), every pinned package from the local SwiftPM cache, every git command traced and every one that named a remote refused (each run's `git-network-check.log`). Run 1 failed only where GRDB's submodule `SQLiteCustom/src` would have been cloned, and at the IdentityProbe. Run 2 left that submodule empty through git's environment configuration (GRDB 7.11.1's `Package.swift` builds nothing from it): `linkage.sh` passed every section, and `resolve-locks.sh` resolved all 15 locks it lists with none changed, its one failure the IdentityProbe, whose expected refusal needs the remotes. `linkage-compare.log` finds no change in which third-party code ships: every symbol count is the same except GRDB's in the host recovery tool (20,313 then, 20,318 now), and the Mac source service now loads five more Apple libraries, which its QR-code pairing code (`PairingQRCode.swift`, merged at M0 assembly) brought.
- **The records:** ADR-0014's status line, sections 1, 2, 3 (its rules, counts, limits and timing), 4 (a sub-bullet for Xcode 27.1), 5 and 6, items 4, 5 and 9, Evidence, Open items, Consequences and Alternatives, each change with a dated note; R4-05, R4-06 and R4-07 recorded with their state; and `Docs/Licenses/third-party-inventory.md`, a dated note on the 27.1 run. The other owners' records were left to them, by request (section 30).

Times are UTC; the hand-over's first logged step ran at 15:49 UTC on 23 October 2026, 04:49 on 24 October on the build Mac's clock, the date its commits carry. These are agent reviews, not a person's, and nothing here is a device result, a role sign-off or the M0 exit.

### 29.1 Where the records came from, and what was committed

- **Source and extraction.** The records are the structured results that the workflow agents of 13-15 October 2026 returned (each item's author, its reviews and its fixes), kept in the journals of the orchestrating session's workflow runs on the build Mac, outside the repository. The orchestrating session extracted them on 24 October 2026 into one JSON file per item and a summary, in its scratchpad folder; the hand-over copied the fourteen files byte for byte and checked them against that folder and by SHA-256. The agent that made the hand-over did not read the journals, which were outside its file access, so that the extraction holds every record the journals hold rests on the extraction.
- **What was committed** (`evidence/M0-Integration/item-reviews/`): the fourteen files as extracted, named `<file>.json.txt`, not `.json`, because `Scripts/audit-hard-rules.sh` reads every `.json` file as configuration and the reviews' prose names keys under rules that can never be allowlisted (`NSAllowsArbitraryLoads` and `kSecAttrAccessibleAlways` among them): named `.json`, five of the files give 23 findings, and named `.json.txt`, none (`audit-probe.log`); one Markdown record per item, generated by `commands/item-reviews.py render`, giving every author, review and fix round in order with its run, commit and verdict, each finding with what became of it, and the final verdict with the findings that stayed open; a README; and the commands (`item-reviews.py`, `audit-probe.sh`, `compare-gate-checks.py`, `run-checks.sh`).
- **The pointers:** one line in each of the eight gate reports, `M0-contracts.md`, ADR-0015, ADR-0016, the runbook's header and Section 9 of the amendments; ADR-0014's, another workstream's file then, was requested of its owner, and added at the assembly (section 30).

### 29.2 What the records show

- **63 records** (13 authors, 35 reviews, 15 fixes) in six workflow runs, with 247 findings in all. Every record's commit is a commit of this repository on its item's branch, and the rounds chain: each review read the commit of the author or fix before it. *(Dated note, 26 October 2026: of this repository on the build Mac alone: the 28 commits the records cite are on the thirteen local `m0-int-*` branches and no other ref, never pushed and held by no tag, so the records' check can pass only there; whether to keep, push or tag them is the owner's (the gate summary's Section 5.4): the second review of the M0 exit preparation, its finding EV-04; section 32.)*
- **Every final round reviewed the commit that was merged:** the final commit 7.1 records, the head of its branch; and the verdicts are 7.1's, twelve accept-with-minor and the deps item's reject.
- **Open when the items were merged:** the 91 findings the final rounds raised, two of them major (the deps item's R4-01 and R4-02, section 28) and 89 minor, none of which but those two was written into any report; and 30 earlier findings without a recorded resolution, 5 left unresolved and 25 named by no later record, because the lists handed to the contracts and amendments items' fixers were cut off (one of the 25 major: the contracts item's L2-03). *(Dated note, 26 October 2026: two clauses of this bullet hold only in part, as the second review of the M0 exit preparation found, its findings EV-01 and EV-02 (section 32). "None of which but those two was written into any report" held at the merge and on `m0-exit-reviews`; on `m0-exit-prep`, since the Zip guard's fix (section 28), ADR-0014 also gives the deps item's R4-03 to R4-07. And the records give the cut-off lists as the reason for the contracts item's 19 of the 25 alone, whose fixers recorded lists they did not receive in full (fix-2's ended partway through L2-R2-01; fix-1 had R1-10's text truncated); for the amendments item's six, review-2b's L2-12 to L2-17, they record only that the round-3 reviews' briefs were cut off at R2-06, not whether fix-2, which names none of them, received them. Nor does the reason reach the five left unresolved: the amendments item's L2-06 and L2-07 were left by its fix-1, whose list was cut off; the contracts item's R2-05, into which R1-11 was carried, was declined by its fix-2 as outside the item's files; and the G0.5 AI item's R7-04 was left as the owner's action.)*
- **Gaps:** the run `wf_84f4e4ba-f4a`, which the gate summary named, holds no extracted record, and `wf_5ce13c9e-6bf`, which it did not name, holds four; finding IDs are reused within two items (the contracts item's L2-01 to L2-09, the amendments item's L2-12 to L2-17), and each record gives each finding with its round; the records carry no times.
- **For the owner, and for the agents of the sessions:** the G0.5 AI item's R8-04, open since the merge: the cleanup command `G05-AI.md` gives (Review round 6, last paragraph) would also delete G0.5 recovery's test root `g05-tests`, and it is unchanged; and the final-round findings on steps the owner will run (G0.1 EPUB R4-03 and R4-04, G0.2 R4-01 to R4-03, G0.3 R5-03, G0.5 AI R8-02, G0.6 R4-05, the runbook item's R3-01 to R3-03), which later rounds may have answered and the hand-over did not check (the gate summary's Section 5.4).

By two agents of the M0 exit preparation, one after the other, each in its own worktree, on branch `m0-exit-prep`, which held `5aec2518` and nothing more: `main` after pull request #6 (`96bdc7e9`), with the `CLAUDE.md` correction that the orchestrating session made on top of it at the product owner's request on 24 October 2026 (a request recorded only in that commit's message, which the session wrote: section 33, HON-EP-03). The first agent merged the two branches on 24 October 2026 (23 October, 17:25 UTC) and made most of the changes below, and stopped at its usage limit before it committed them, wrote this section or ran the checks; the orchestrating session committed its uncommitted changes as they stood, unreviewed, as `83ed5ff3` (26 October 2026 on the build Mac's clock, 25 October, 16:05 UTC). The second agent, the continuation, started from that commit on 26 October 2026 (25 October UTC): it checked each change against the requests and the records, corrected what did not hold (30.3), wrote this section, the assembly's checks and their README, and ran the checks (30.5). The dated notes each agent added carry the day it wrote them: 24 October 2026 for the first, 26 October for the continuation. Agent-written; not reviewed; human sign-off pending. Host only: the build Mac, macOS 26.6.2 (25G83), Apple M5 Max, Xcode 27.1 (27A9275). No simulator was created or used, no device was contacted, nothing played audio, and nothing was pushed or fetched; neither agent read Apple's pages, which the orchestrating session read and relayed (30.3). The agent sandbox refused `source` at the prompt, so every script run here sources `Scripts/lib/env.sh` itself. Nothing here is a device result, a role sign-off, an ADR approval or the M0 exit.

## 31. The review of the M0 exit preparation, and its fix (26 October 2026)

An agent review read branch `m0-exit-prep` at `609ed492`, the assembly's last commit (section 30), and the workflow relayed two findings, as the complete list: SEC-ZIPGUARD-01 (major) and SEC-ZIPGUARD-02 (minor). Which lenses reviewed the branch was not relayed, nor whether the review judged R4-01 and R4-02 answered, so round 4's verdict on ADR-0014 stays on the record; the review's own reproduction scripts are in its worktree, which the fix did not read, and no record of the review is committed beyond what this section, the gate summary and the fix's evidence README record. The fix is by an agent, on `m0-exit-prep`, in its own worktree, from `609ed492`; agent-written, not reviewed, human sign-off pending. *(Dated note, 26 October 2026: read by a second agent review the same day, at `83030fba`: 31.5 and section 32.)* Host only: the build Mac, macOS 26.6.2 (25G83), Apple M5 Max, Xcode 27.1 (27A9275). No simulator was created or used, no device was contacted, nothing played audio, and nothing was pushed or fetched. Times are UTC: the fix's first logged step, the LFS checkout, ran at 17:48 UTC on 25 October 2026, 06:48 on 26 October on the build Mac's clock, the date its commits and dated notes carry. The worktree's 777 Git LFS files were pointer files again; the follow-up's `commands/lfs-checkout.sh`, unchanged, found every object in the local store and filled them from it, with no download (`evidence/M0-ExitPrep/fix-1/lfs-checkout.log`). The agent sandbox refused `source` at the prompt, so every script run here sources `Scripts/lib/env.sh` itself. Outside `Docs/` the fix changed `Scripts/check-dependency-lock.py` and nothing else.

### 31.1 The findings, and what was done

| Finding | Severity | What was done |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SEC-ZIPGUARD-01: the Zip guard's `check_product_manifest` failed a product manifest's local package only under `Spikes/` or outside the product roots; it never applied the rule that every other path a product manifest names inside `Apps/` and `Packages/` must hold what git tracks (`judge_inside`, since review round 3, R3-01). So a product `project.yml` or `Package.swift` could depend on a package in a package's own `.build/checkouts`, or in a directory under the roots that holds only untracked files, and the check passed; and a target's dependency on that package's product was not judged either, since only remote packages have an inventory role. ADR-0014's rule 5 says that product code comes only from what git tracks under the product roots, its section 3 names local packages among the routes the guard judges, and the gate summary's row 76 gives the guard as closing the routes of that kind. The review reproduced it as b00, b02 and b03, with b01, the same package outside the roots, as its control, which the check reported; XcodeGen refuses a local package whose directory is absent and takes one populated at build time, and no product manifest used the route | major | The check now fails a product manifest's local package inside the product roots whose `Package.swift` is not a file git tracks there (`outside-product`, naming the package and, where it is one, the build or package-checkout directory), whichever targets use it. A tracked local package's manifest is one the check reads as a product manifest, and its sources are files it scans, so the products it provides are judged by the product rules; that answers the finding's note that a dependency on such a product had no role check, since an inventory role is for remote packages, and a local package's code is judged as product code instead. The guard's change, `ad709059`, adds five mutations to the self-test (the review's three routes, XcodeGen's `localPackages` form, and a tracked directory with no `Package.swift`, since tracked content alone is not enough) and one allowed change (a product package that depends on another by a path inside the roots, whose manifest git tracks): 283 mutations, 36 allowed changes, all 120 failure branches reached. `evidence/M0-ExitPrep/fix-1/reproductions.log` plants six routes in copies of the tree at `609ed492`, the review's three among them and one in a copy made a git repository (a package git does not track outside any `.build`): each passed the check before the change and fails it after with exactly the new finding, the review's b01 failing both times; and it shows SwiftPM reading such a manifest with the package's directory absent, XcodeGen refusing the package while its directory is absent and taking it once populated, and Xcode compiling a stand-in package there and linking it into a probe tool. ADR-0014's rule 5, section 3 (its routes and the self-test's counts), status, Evidence, a consequence and the security reviewer's open item, the ADR index's row, the check's docstring, and the gate summary's row 76 (with its checker), Sections 1, 2.10 and 6.1, item 4, the Branch line and item 111 record it; the repository passes unchanged |
| SEC-ZIPGUARD-02: the gate summary's item 22, on the fix of the re-pin round's review (TC-02, TC-08), said in the present tense that the self-test "has 239 mutations (228 before) ... and reaches all 111 of its failure branches", its counts of 20 October 2026, while row 76 and ADR-0014 gave 278; no outcome changed | minor | Item 22 gives them in the past tense, with a dated note on the counts since (278 and 119 from the fix of R4-01 and R4-02, 283 and 120 from this fix); row 76's "with a self-test that plants each route (278 mutations)" and item 105's "its self-test plants each one (278 mutations)", which gave the same kind of count in the present tense, likewise; item 112 records it |

**The review status of the exit preparation.** With the review, the records that gave the exit preparation as not reviewed now say that an agent review read it at `609ed492`, and that this fix has not been reviewed: the gate summary's State line (199 words, within the fourth fix's limit of 200), its Section 1 bullet on the preparation, Section 6.2 (a row for the review, before the hand-over's row, which stays the table's last, and a note on the paragraph after the table), items 109 (a dated note) and 113, Section 7's item 5 and Section 8 (notes on the bullets of the Zip guard's fix, the hand-over and the assembly, a bullet for this fix, and a note on the Written by line); 30.6 here; the Stage 0 README; the runbook's Who wrote it, this fix's only change to the runbook; and the evidence READMEs of the Zip guard's fix and of the assembly. Each is a dated note, except the State line's clause, "nor has the M0 exit preparation of 24 October 2026", which is now "nor has the fix of the review of the M0 exit preparation": the line has room for no note, and the assembly's recheck pins the old clause, so it reports its group R6 there, by design (31.4).

### 32.1 The findings, and what was done

| Finding | Severity | What was done |

## 34. The fourth review of the M0 exit preparation, and its fix (26 October 2026)

An agent review read branch `m0-exit-prep` at `fcbd6c89`, the last commit of the fix of the third review (section 33), and the workflow relayed one finding, as the complete list: SEC-ZIPGUARD-03 (major), on the Zip guard and ADR-0014's account of it: the route around the guard that the first fix's agent found and left open (31.5), a version-specific manifest beside a product `Package.swift`, which ADR-0014 did not name while it said that a product `Package.swift` reads nothing but its own text. The review reproduced it on a copy of the tree, as the first fix's observation had, and noted that the gate summary disclosed the route as open, so that it was not a hidden bypass but ADR-0014, the guard's authoritative record, overstated its coverage. Which lenses reviewed the branch was not relayed, nor whether the review judged R4-01 and R4-02 answered, so round 4's verdict on ADR-0014 stays on the record; the review's own reproduction script is in its worktree, which the fix did not read, and no record of the review is committed beyond what this section, the gate summary and the fix's evidence README record. The finding's citations were read where it places them, at `fcbd6c89` (`evidence/M0-ExitPrep/fix-2/finding-lines.log`). The fix is by an agent, on `m0-exit-prep`, in its own worktree, from `fcbd6c89`; agent-written, not reviewed, human sign-off pending. *(Dated note, 26 October 2026: read by a fifth agent review the same day, at `941ac9a2`: 34.5 and section 35.)* Host only: the build Mac, macOS 26.6.2 (25G83), Apple M5 Max, Xcode 27.1 (27A9275). No simulator was created or used, no device was contacted, nothing played audio, and nothing was pushed or fetched; no branch, tag or other ref was made or changed but the fix's own commits on `m0-exit-prep`. Times are UTC: the fix's first logged step, the LFS checkout, ran at 21:53 UTC on 25 October 2026, 10:53 on 26 October on the build Mac's clock, the date its commits and dated notes carry. The worktree's 777 Git LFS files were pointer files again; the follow-up's `commands/lfs-checkout.sh`, unchanged, found every object in the local store and filled them from it, with no download (`evidence/M0-ExitPrep/fix-2/lfs-checkout.log`). ### 34.1 The finding, and what was done

| Finding | Severity | What was done |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SEC-ZIPGUARD-03: the guard builds a product `Package.swift`'s model from `swift package dump-package`, which evaluates a version-specific manifest (`Package@swift-<version>.swift`) beside it instead, when one exists and the toolchain's version allows; but the manifest-input rule, review round 4's R4-01 for SwiftPM, read the raw text of `Package.swift` alone, and `check_manifests` read no other file as a manifest. So a product package could carry a version-specific manifest that reads the environment, a clock or randomness, or holds an `#if`, and give a build with the variable set a source path into `Spikes/`, an implicit `-import-module` or `-include`, a test-only package or a conditional dependency that CI, without the variable, never saw: R4-01's mechanism. The review planted `Packages/Design/Package@swift-6.4.swift` taking the SylviaDesign target's path from `SYLVIA_EXTRA`, `dump-package` followed the variable and the check reported 0 failures. ADR-0014's rule 5 said that a product `Package.swift` reads nothing but its own text, its section 5, on which the security sign-off of item 4 rests, that CI reads every value a product manifest gives as the build uses it, and its list of what the check cannot see did not name the route; the gate summary's row 76 and item 113 disclosed it as open, for ADR-0014's owner and the next review | major | The guard, `4d72eeca`, fails every file under the product roots whose name begins with `Package@` and ends with `.swift`, in any letter case (`manifest-input`): a product package keeps one manifest, `Package.swift`, the one the rule reads, whatever SwiftPM's rule for choosing among such files, which `evidence/M0-ExitPrep/fix-2/reproductions.log`, part 2a, shows under twenty names (it matched `PACKAGE@SWIFT-6.4.SWIFT` too). The cache of `dump-package` results is keyed by every file SwiftPM could take as the manifest. Reading every name the check reads as it spells it against the tools, since SwiftPM matched that name in any letter case, found routes of the same kind on the default macOS file system, which ignores letter case: a path tracked under `packages/`, which git checks out into `Packages/` and SwiftPM compiles there, and the check read as neither product nor spike code; manifests named `package.swift` or `Project.yml`, which SwiftPM and XcodeGen load and the check did not read; and module maps named `MODULE.MODULEMAP`, `MODULE.MAP` or `module_private.map`, which clang loads and the check did not read. The guard now fails a root or a manifest named in another letter case (new category `letter-case`) and reads module maps by every name clang reads them by; a `.SWIFT` file is compiled by neither SwiftPM nor an XcodeGen project, so the import scan stays as it was. The self-test has eight mutations and two allowed changes more: 291 mutations, 38 allowed changes, 24 categories, all 123 failure branches reached. The reproductions plant thirteen routes in copies of the tree at `fcbd6c89`: nine passed the check before the change and fail it after, every finding at a planted file, among them the review's route, the same in capitals, and one that adds the test-only SnapshotTesting to SylviaDesign from the environment, which the check before the change reports with the variable set; the four controls, each the same change with every name as the check reads it, fail both times, the same way. ADR-0014's rule 5, section 3 (the manifest-input rule, a bullet for the letter-case rule, the counts, and the limits, which now name a file a tool reads under a name the check does not know, a later toolchain's say), section 5 (a dated note on the sentence the finding cites), section 6 (a toolchain change runs the reproductions again), status, Evidence, a consequence and the security reviewer's open item describe it; the gate summary's row 76 (its clause on the route reworded, with a note), with its checker, Sections 1, 2.10 and 6.1, item 4's bullet, items 22, 105, 111 and 113 (dated notes), items 125 and 126, and Section 8; the ADR index's row; the Stage 0 README; 31.5, 32.2, 32.5, 33.2 and 33.5 here; and the evidence READMEs of the first three fixes and the Zip guard's fix (dated notes). No product package has such a file, so the repository passes unchanged and no status changes: row 76 stays failed |

**The review status of the third fix.** With the review, the records that gave the fix of the third review as not reviewed now say that this fourth review read it at `fcbd6c89`: the gate summary's State line, whose clause "nor has the fix of the third review of the M0 exit preparation" is now "nor has the fix of the fourth review of the M0 exit preparation" (200 words, within the fourth fix's limit of 200), Section 6.2 (the third review's row, a row for the fourth, before the hand-over's row, which stays the table's last, and a note on the paragraph after the table), items 109, 113, 118 and 124, Section 7's item 5 and Section 8 (the third fix's bullet, a bullet for this fix, the Branch line and the Written by line); with item 4's bullet on R4-01 and R4-02, Section 1's bullet on the preparation, row 76 and Section 6.1, which SEC-ZIPGUARD-03 changes too; ADR-0014's status, the ADR index's row of ADR-0014, the Stage 0 README, the runbook's Who wrote it (this fix's only change to the runbook), 30.6, 31.5, section 32 (its opening and 32.5) and section 33 (its opening and 33.5) here, and the evidence READMEs of the third fix, the second fix, the first fix, the assembly, the Zip guard's fix and the hand-over's two. Each is a dated note, except the State line's clause and Section 6.2's cell for the third review, reworded: the line has room for no note, and the third fix's recheck pins both, and row 76's clause on the route, so it reports its groups H1 and R there, by design (34.4).

### 36.2 The device-A change: the decision, and where each record changed

The owner wrote: "iphone 11 pro seems to be no longer charging. i do have a 13 mini that is still working, so update the plan. even better from a screen size." Told that the iPhone 13 mini lays out at the same 375 × 812 points as the iPhone 11 Pro, and that its A15 is two generations newer than the A13, the slowest chip iOS 26 supports, the owner chose "13 mini only (Recommended)": "The 13 mini becomes device A. The records say plainly that the slowest supported chip (A13) isn't measured on a device. That's left for M8's release device matrix, with a borrowed or bought A13 phone if wanted. Simplest, and no extra session time." (`evidence/M0-DeviceA/README.md` gives the whole exchange and the two options not chosen.) So D-1, part (i), was taken again by the owner, and the records add the decision to D-1 of 22 October 2026 as a dated addendum, keeping that record as history: device A, the baseline for small-screen layout on a device and not the oldest supported iPhone, is the iPhone 13 mini; the steps that depend on its screen are covered as on the iPhone 11 Pro (the same 375 × 812 pt, measured on the simulator, drawn about 8% smaller); the figures that depend on its chip, memory, heat or storage are the iPhone 13 mini's, never an A13 phone's, and no budget changes; the A13 floor is left for M8's release device matrix, where the Section 19.2 budgets are to be measured on an A13 iPhone, the owner's iPhone 11 if it still works then, or one borrowed or bought if the owner wants one. D-2 (a) stands: the exit waits for Session 8 on device A; the narrower form of D-2 (b)'s exception, that the chip-dependent results on device A are not A13 results, is recorded where (b) said an exception goes, each as far as it applies. `evidence/M0-DeviceA/inventory-records.md` lists every mention this part found, with its disposition.

#### 36.8.5 What fails by design, and why

Each part's checks were written for its own tree; on the tree that merges both they report the other part's changes, and the assembly's checks replace them. The instructions part's gate checks fail in parts 1a and 1b (they allowed its own files only and required the plan, the ADRs, the reports, this log and `README.md` unchanged), part 3 (one path the gate summary cites, the log of the assembly's own gate checks, which run last) and part 4: the follow-up's fix's amendments checker, which they required to pass unchanged and which fails on the three checks whose anchors the records part replaced (its successor passes); the earlier rechecks, which also report FID4-04 and HON4-06; and their recheck's floor item, on M8's wording. The records part's runner fails in step 5 (the earlier rechecks also report what the instructions part changed: FID4-02, HON4-01, OWN4-03, OWN4-06, OWN5-02 and the drop-in record's two texts, and recheck-unl's UNL-02 line now gives the State line as 198 words) and step 6 (the decisions round's runbook content check reports the three texts the instructions part changed; that part's successor passes). The instructions part's recheck fails on its floor item; the assembly's recheck asserts the new wording.

#### 36.8.6 Seen at the assembly: the sessions' checkout on `m0-decisions`

When the assembly read the branches, read-only (`evidence/M0-DeviceA/assembly/checks/branch-state.log`, whose header gives the time; the gate checks' part 1i read them again later), `git worktree list` showed `~/Git/sylvia-sessions` on `m0-decisions`, not on `main` at `96bdc7e9` as 36.1 has it. `m0-decisions` then had commits after `044ed3cf`, the commit pull request #6 merged, made from 06:12 that morning on the build Mac's clock, and more were added while the assembly ran; their messages record device runs and the sessions' setup (the first, "Record the first physical iPhone Keychain test result"). Among other files they change the session log, the runbook, the G0.5 recovery checklist, the G0.2, G0.5 AI and G0.5 recovery reports and the license inventory, add device evidence under `evidence/` (G0.2, G0.4, G0.5 AI, G0.5 recovery and the runbook's folder), and add `Spikes/G05-Recovery/DeviceTests`, which lies in the paths runbook 0.8 checks; four of their files are files this update changes too (the runbook, the G0.5 recovery checklist, the G0.2 and the G0.5 recovery reports). The log lists the commits and files as they stood when it was written. The assembly read only the commits' metadata and their file list: it read, recorded and inferred none of their results, and changed no record for them. So this update's instructions (the device sessions built from `m0-sessions` in that checkout; `m0-decisions` behind `main`, never to build from; the session log with no sitting of the owner's on 24 October 2026) do not describe that checkout as it now is. Reconciling `m0-decisions` with `m0-sessions` before `~/Git/sylvia-sessions` moves is the orchestrating session's, with the owner; a merge of those commits into `m0-sessions` would change `Spikes/G05-Recovery`, so 0.8's check would then call for 0.8 to run again. *(The same day the fix of the update's review brought the owner's instructions in line with that state, and left the reconciliation to the orchestrating session, with the owner: 37.1, 37.2.)*

### 37.2 The reconciliation of `m0-decisions` with `m0-sessions`, and why this fix did not make it

*(26 October 2026: made the other way round: `m0-records-update`, made from `m0-decisions`, merged `m0-sessions` into it, and `m0-decisions` stays the session branch: section 41.)*

FID-DA-01 asks that `m0-decisions` be reconciled with `m0-sessions` before the sessions' checkout moves. This fix did not merge `m0-decisions`, for four reasons. The owner's sittings may still be running: the last commit there, at 17:33 UTC on 25 October 2026, about an hour before this fix began, is titled "Record setup checks and the audio device run in progress", and the runbook merges into the branch the sessions build from only between sittings. The merge brings the owner's own records and results into these records: the session log's rows, device evidence, and changed rows of the G0.2, G0.5 AI and G0.5 recovery reports, whose statuses the gate summary's Section 2 copies, so that the gate summary, the runbook's header and the Stage 0 README would then have to record them; committing a sitting's records is the owner's, or an agent's the owner asks (the runbook's 0.3, step 6), and no such request was relayed. After it, 0.8 runs again (`DeviceTests` under its paths), on the build Mac with its screen unlocked, which no agent arranges, and the license inventory's new lock (`Spikes/G05-Recovery/DeviceTests/Package.resolved`) goes through `12-dependency-lock`. And phone B's iOS build there, 27.0.1 (24A446), differs from the 26.7 (23H24) these records give (the runbook's 1.1, ADR-0001, the G0.4 and G0.5 recovery checklists), which the reconciliation settles with the owner. What the merge needs, read-only (`branch-state.log`): the 8 commits after `044ed3cf` change 25 files, four of which this update also changed (the runbook, the G0.5 recovery checklist, and the G0.2 and G0.5 recovery reports); merged into this fix's head they give no conflict. Then, in order: run 0.8's check (it lists `DeviceTests`), run 0.8 with the screen unlocked, record the sittings' results where each record copies them, settle phone B's build, apply the session log's rows 1 and 8 that 36.8.2 asks, and switch the checkout to `m0-sessions` once this update's review is done. The orchestrating session does it, with the owner, between sittings; until then the owner's instructions say to leave the checkout on `m0-decisions` and keep each sitting's records there (the runbook's 0.3, step 1). *(26 October 2026: the fix of the same review's completeness findings found that neither the drop-in nor the lock harness's checks builds, copies or runs `DeviceTests`, and runbook 0.8's rule now sets it apart, so in the order above 0.8's check still runs after the merge, but the drop-in's run on `9cdb0a02` stands for `DeviceTests`, and 0.8 is not run again for it unless the merge brings anything else under 0.8's paths: 39.1, DA-07. The merge also brings the runbook's commands that run the recovery suite on the phone through `DeviceTests`: 39.1, DA-01.)*

### 37.3 What this fix left as it was

### 41.3 The branch model, and run 1

`m0-decisions` is the product owner's session branch: the owner builds the device sessions from it, in `~/Git/sylvia-sessions`, and commits the session records on it. Agent work goes on a branch of its own made from `m0-decisions` (this one; `m0-exit-prep` and `m0-sessions` were made before that rule was restated, from `5aec2518`, whose code is that of `044ed3cf`) and reaches the owner's build only when merged into `m0-decisions` between sittings, by the owner or by an agent the owner asks. `m0-sessions` is retired: its work arrives through this branch, and nothing is built from it. Nothing reaches `main` before the M0 exit unless the owner decides otherwise: the owner's answer of 23 October 2026 extended H-1's exception to pull request #5 and the merge of `m0-decisions`, and pull request #6 made that merge (ADR-0002, Branches). ## 42. The fidelity findings of the review of `m0-records-update`, and their fix (26 October 2026)

By an agent, on `m0-records-update` from `832a3636`, its last commit, which an agent review read, in a worktree of its own, at the workflow's request of 26 October 2026, which relayed the review's fidelity findings, FID-RU-01 to FID-RU-11: one blocker, two major and eight minor (`evidence/M0-RecordsUpdate/fix-1/relayed-findings.md`, verbatim). The review's other lenses' findings go to fixes that follow on the same branch, one at a time; none was relayed here. Agent-written; not reviewed; human sign-off pending. Host only: the build Mac, macOS 26.6.2 (25G83), Apple M5 Max, Xcode 27.1 (27A9275). No simulator was created or used, no device was contacted, nothing played audio, nothing was pushed or fetched, and no ref was written but this branch's commits: `m0-decisions`, `m0-exit-prep` and `m0-sessions` were read, never committed to, reset or checked out, and `~/Git/sylvia-sessions`, where the owner and Codex work on `m0-decisions`, was not touched. Times are UTC: the worktree checked out `m0-records-update` at 07:55 UTC on 26 October 2026 (20:55 on the build Mac's clock), and its 777 Git LFS files were filled from the repository's local LFS store with `git lfs checkout`, with no download. Nothing here is a device result, a role sign-off, an ADR approval (decision D-6) or the M0 exit.

### 42.2 `CLAUDE.md`: the second of the review's two fixes, and why

The review left two fixes: the owner applies the paragraph, or asks for it, in the same sitting as the merge and before any request to an agent; or the merge leaves `m0-decisions`'s `CLAUDE.md` unchanged. The first rests on a step the owner could miss, and between the merge and that step every agent asked to work from `m0-decisions` would read a paragraph that says no one should build on it; the second needs no step. So `CLAUDE.md` on this branch is `m0-decisions`'s, byte for byte as at `c72247a6` (`git diff c72247a6 -- CLAUDE.md` prints nothing), the paragraph of 22 October 2026 as `d076f6fe` left it. No text is written in it: the file the owner has on `m0-decisions` stays exactly as the owner has it, so the rule that no agent changes `CLAUDE.md`, the instructions agents follow, on a workflow's request (the gate summary's item 46) is kept, as the review found the integration's refusal to rewrite the paragraph kept it. What stays out of date on `m0-decisions` is what item 46 describes: the paragraph says that the gate branches and the M0 items are combined on `m0-integrate`. So the owner's step 2 stays: the texts of `evidence/M0-RecordsUpdate/claude-md-paragraph.md` replace that paragraph and the toolchain line, which is the same on both (its pin unchanged, `12-dependency-lock` passing), as they would have replaced `6cede84a`'s; until then the owner names `m0-decisions` as the base in every request. A merge of this branch can no longer conflict in `CLAUDE.md` if the owner applies the texts first. The paragraphs `5aec2518` and `6cede84a` wrote at the owner's request of 24 October 2026 stay in this branch's history, on `m0-exit-prep` and `m0-sessions`, with the commit messages that record that request.

### 42.3 The check FID-RU-01 asked for

`evidence/M0-RecordsUpdate/fix-1/commands/retired-branch-texts.py` reads every live text: `CLAUDE.md`, the repository README, the plan, every Markdown file of `Docs/ADRs`, `Docs/Stage0` and `Docs/Stage0/device-checklists`, and this branch's evidence READMEs (not the findings as relayed, which are quoted verbatim). The other evidence folders record earlier runs and rounds and are not edited; and two kinds of record inside the live files are read as history by design: the gate summary's Section 6.3 groups of the rounds before `m0-records-update` and its Section 8 bullets on them, and this log's sections 1-40, each the record of a round as it was done. For every mention of `m0-sessions` in code marks (and of `git checkout m0-sessions`) it takes its clause (between the nearest sentence end, semicolon or parenthesis on each side, the parentheticals inside left out; a wrapped paragraph joined first) and judges it a branch use when it matches a list of uses (build from, commit on, merge into, go into or reach, move to, made from, start or work from, check out or switch to, is or continues on, plays the part, is the session branch, holds the session records, use). A branch use is history when its clause is a quotation or has a mark of history (until a date or a round, from one date to another, a text that said or had, as written then, a past passive, a past event with its date, retired or dropped, rejected or moved, a finding's words, a report of what a text says); superseded when a later note of 26 October 2026 in the same paragraph says that `m0-sessions` is retired or `m0-decisions` the session branch again; and live otherwise, which fails the check. It lists every branch use with how it read it, so that a reader can audit the reading; with `--all` it lists every mention. On `832a3636`'s tree it fails on eleven clauses: `CLAUDE.md`'s two, ADR-0002's three, the runbook's 0.7 and the gate summary's five (5.4's two, and the notes of 42.1's FID-RU-04 row); on this fix's tree it passes; a copy of this tree with 0.7's old record put back fails on that clause alone. It is a reading of words, not of meaning: a live use phrased in none of its forms would pass, which the listing of every branch use, and of every mention with `--all`, lets a reader look for.

### 42.7 What the earlier checks report now, by design

Each pins texts of the branch it was written on, and this fix changed some of them on purpose; beside what `m0-records-update` recorded of each (41.7):

- **`CLAUDE.md`, back to the paragraph of 22 October 2026**: `m0-exit-prep`'s gate checks read it in their part 1 and in the rechecks of HON-11, and now find that it no longer names `main`, no longer says never to build on `m0-decisions`, and again says that the work is combined on `m0-integrate`, so they report HON-11 open, left to the owner, where they reported it changed; `recheck-hon-ep.py` reports one more text of its group B not as stated (`CLAUDE.md` as `5aec2518` left it) and, in its sweep, that listed text gone and this fix's account of `6cede84a`'s paragraph (the gate summary's item 189) among the unlisted texts; `m0-sessions`'s gate checks report `CLAUDE.md` with no claim that `m0-decisions` is behind `main`.
- **The opening's words**: the rechecks of OWN5-05 count 596 words and 769 with the State line (593 and 766 before), each within its limit, and report the State line's pointer as before.
- **This fix's new texts and files**: the sweeps of the earlier rechecks read this fix's Markdown files too, find some of this fix's notes among their phrasings (for example ADR-0002's note that R4-01 and R4-02 are closed in the guard) and list them as unlisted; part 1 of each gate check counts this fix's files among what changed since its own start; and the gate summary's line numbers after Section 6.2 are one more (a row added there).
- **What does not change**: every check that must pass passes, and every one that must fail by design fails on exactly what it failed on at `m0-records-update`, but for `m0-records-update`'s amendments checker, whose pointer this fix changed (42.6).
